## GHSA-cxjq-mrr5-89rv · github.com/traefik/traefik

2026-08-06 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The Traefik ReplacePathRegex middleware did not properly sanitize or normalize URL paths after modification. An attacker could craft a URL with path traversal sequences (e.g., `../`) that, after the regex replacement, would bypass authentication or authorization rules by pointing to an unintended internal path.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication and authorization mechanisms, gaining unauthorized access to internal services or resources that would otherwise be protected.

### Key diff

```
- 	currentPath := req.URL.EscapedPath()
+ 		// Stop here if the normalization of the path produces a different path.
+ 		if path != req.URL.Path {
+ 			logger.Debugf("Rejecting request, sanitized path: %q is not equivalent to stripped path: %q", path, req.URL.Path)
+ 			http.Error(rw, http.StatusText(http.StatusBadRequest), http.StatusBadRequest)
+ 			return
+ 		}
 	}
 
 	rp.next.ServeHTTP(rw, req)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a normalization step using `req.URL.JoinPath()` after the path replacement. It then compares the original path with the normalized path and rejects the request if they differ, preventing path traversal attacks.

## GHSA-fgjj-px3w-67xx · github.com/traefik/traefik/v3

2026-08-06 · Go · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insufficient uniqueness in the naming scheme for HTTPRoute and GRPCRoute objects. The `routeKey` and subsequent `routerName` were constructed without including the namespace of the backend service, leading to potential collisions. An attacker could craft a route in their own namespace that would hijack traffic intended for a legitimate service in another namespace if the route names and other identifying factors (excluding namespace) happened to collide.

### Impact

An attacker could hijack traffic for services in other namespaces, potentially leading to data interception, denial of service, or unauthorized access to backend services.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/provider/kubernetes/gateway/kubernetes.go
+++ b/pkg/provider/kubernetes/gateway/kubernetes.go
@@ -1201,14 +1202,21 @@ func matchListener(listener gatewayListener, parentRef gatev1.ParentReference) b
 	return true
 }
 
-func makeRouterName(rule, name string) string {
+func makeRouterName(kind, rule, namespace, name, gatewayNamespace, gatewayName, epName string, ruleIndex int) string {
+	label := provider.Normalize(fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s-%s-gw-%s-%s-ep-%s-%d", kind, namespace, name, gatewayNamespace, gatewayName, epName, ruleIndex))
+
 	h := sha256.New()
 
+	for _, c := range []string{namespace, name, gatewayNamespace, gatewayName, epName, strconv.Itoa(ruleIndex)} {
+		// Length-prefixing to avoid ambiguity between distinct components with embedded delimiter.
+		fmt.Fprintf(h, "%d:%s", len(c), c)
+	}
+
 	// As explained in https://pkg.go.dev/hash#Hash,
 	// Write never returns an error.
 	h.Write([]byte(rule))
 
-	return fmt.Sprintf("%s-%.10x", name, h.Sum(nil))
+	return fmt.Sprintf("%s-%.10x", label, h.Sum(nil))
 }
 
 func getTLSConfig(tlsConfigs map[string]*tls.CertAndStores) []*tls.CertAndStores {
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `makeRouterName` function to include the namespace of the HTTPRoute/GRPCRoute and the gateway, along with a length-prefixed SHA256 hash of these components, ensuring a globally unique identifier for each router. Additionally, the `loadService` function now incorporates the router name and backend index into the service name to prevent collisions for backend references.

## GHSA-93qh-5269-9wcf · statamic/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The system allowed matching an OAuth user to an existing local user account solely based on email address, without verifying if the email address provided by the OAuth provider was actually owned by the user. This meant an attacker could register with an OAuth provider using a victim's email address and then log in, taking over the victim's account.

### Impact

An attacker could take over any existing user account by registering with an OAuth provider using the victim's email address, leading to full compromise of the victim's account.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/OAuth/Provider.php
+++ b/src/OAuth/Provider.php
@@ -62,16 +62,24 @@ public function findOrCreateUser($socialite): StatamicUser
      */
     public function findUser($socialite): ?StatamicUser
     {
-        if (
-            ($user = User::findByOAuthId($this, $socialite->getId())) ||
-            ($user = User::findByEmail($socialite->getEmail()))
-        ) {
+        if ($user = User::findByOAuthId($this, $socialite->getId())) {
+            return $user;
+        }
+
+        if ($this->trustsEmails() && $user = User::findByEmail($socialite->getEmail())) {
             return $user;
         }
 
         return null;
     }
 
+    private function trustsEmails(): bool
+    {
+        return in_array($this->name, config('statamic.oauth.trusted_providers', [
+            'google', 'github', 'apple', 'bitbucket', 'slack', 'slack-openid', 'twitter-oauth-2',
+        ]));
+    }
+
     /**
      * Create a Statamic user from a Socialite user.
      *
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `trustsEmails()` method to explicitly define which OAuth providers are considered trustworthy for email-based user matching. Additionally, it prevents the creation of a new user account via OAuth if an existing user with the same email already exists, unless the provider is trusted.

## GHSA-2q4p-g7hv-5rgv · league/commonmark

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from inefficient handling of multibyte characters in `league/commonmark`'s `Cursor` class. Specifically, operations like `mb_substr` were repeatedly called from the beginning of the string for each character in a sequence, leading to quadratic time complexity (O(n^2)) when processing long lines with many multibyte characters, especially during whitespace scanning.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Markdown input containing a long line with many multibyte characters, causing the parser to consume excessive CPU resources and time. This would lead to a denial of service, making the application unresponsive or crashing it.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Parser/Cursor.php
+++ b/src/Parser/Cursor.php
@@ -53,7 +53,31 @@ class Cursor
     /** @psalm-readonly */
     private bool $isMultibyte;
 
-    /** @var array<int, string> */
+    /**
+     * Lazily-built lookup table mapping a character index to the byte offset at which
+     * that character begins within $line (plus a terminal entry, at index $length,
+     * equal to the total byte length). Only populated for multibyte lines, and only
+     * ever extended forwards, so building it costs O(n) in total across a line while
+     * every individual lookup is amortized O(1). This integer map is what keeps
+     * character<->byte translation - and therefore every substring accessor - linear
+     * rather than O(n^2) on multibyte lines, without materialising the whole line as
+     * an array of per-character strings.
+     *
+     * @var array<int, int>
+     */
+    private array $byteMap = [];
+
+    /** Largest character index whose byte offset has been recorded in $byteMap. */
+    private int $byteMapKnown = 0;
+
+    /**
+     * Small cache of individually-read single characters on multibyte lines, keyed by
+     * character index. Only populated for characters actually read one-at-a-time (e.g.
+     * repeated peek() during delimiter scanning), so it stays tiny; substring accessors
+     * bypass it entirely. Avoids re-slicing the same character on every lookup.
+     *
+     * @var array<int, string>
+     */
     private array $charCache = [];
 
     /**
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a lazy-built `byteMap` to efficiently translate character indices to byte offsets for multibyte strings, ensuring O(1) amortized lookup time. It also optimizes character access by using a `charAt` cache and directly scanning single-byte ASCII characters (like spaces and tabs) at the byte level, avoiding repeated `mb_substr` calls and reducing complexity to O(n).

## GHSA-g2gp-3wwq-f4ph · league/commonmark

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient loop in the `findTargetAndDirection` method when processing adjacent inline attribute blocks. Specifically, when an `AttributesInline` node was encountered and its `isBlock()` method returned true, the loop would continue iterating unnecessarily, leading to excessive CPU consumption for certain crafted inputs.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious Markdown input that, when parsed by the `league/commonmark` library, would cause the application to consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Extension/Attributes/Event/AttributesListener.php
+++ b/src/Extension/Attributes/Event/AttributesListener.php
@@ -94,7 +94,16 @@ private static function findTargetAndDirection($node): array
             }
 
             if ($node instanceof AttributesInline && ($previous === null || ($previous instanceof AbstractInline && $node->isBlock()))) {
-                continue;
+                if (! $node->parent() instanceof FencedCode) {
+                    $target    = $node->parent();
+                    $direction = self::DIRECTION_SUFFIX;
+                }
+
+                break;
             }
 
             if ($previous !== null && ! self::isAttributesNode($previous)) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `break` statement within the loop when an `AttributesInline` node is encountered and determined to be a block. This prevents further unnecessary iterations, optimizing the processing of such nodes and mitigating the CPU exhaustion issue. It also ensures that the parent node is not a `FencedCode` block before setting the target.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 12 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Java, Erlang, RUST, PHP
> → GHSA-4rc3-7j7w-m548
> → GHSA-grgv-6hw6-v9g4
> → GHSA-rwm7-x88c-3g2p

> → GHSA-5xf7-4p34-54qr

## GHSA-jfm3-95jq-q3rf · league/commonmark

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the markdown parser's handling of duplicate footnote definitions. When multiple footnote definitions shared the same label, the system would process each definition independently, leading to redundant creation of 'backreferences'. This unbounded processing of attacker-controlled input (both the number of duplicate definitions and the number of references to them) caused a significant performance degradation.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious markdown input with numerous duplicate footnote definitions and references. Parsing this input would consume excessive CPU and memory resources, leading to a denial of service for the application or server processing the markdown.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Extension/Footnote/Event/GatherFootnotesListener.php
+++ b/src/Extension/Footnote/Event/GatherFootnotesListener.php
@@ -30,14 +30,42 @@ final class GatherFootnotesListener implements ConfigurationAwareInterface
 
     public function onDocumentParsed(DocumentParsedEvent $event): void
     {
-        $document  = $event->getDocument();
-        $footnotes = [];
-
+        $document    = $event->getDocument();
+        $footnotes   = [];
+        $definitions = [];
+        $discarded   = [];
+
+        /** @var array<string, Reference[]> $backrefs */
+        $backrefs = $document->data->get('footnote/backrefs', []);
+
+        /*
+         * A label may be defined more than once. Only the first definition is used, matching how
+         * duplicate link reference definitions are resolved; the rest are discarded below.
+         *
+         * Keeping just one definition per label is also what bounds the work done here: every
+         * definition sharing a label claims that label's entire backref list, so N duplicate
+         * definitions of a label referenced M times would otherwise produce M * N backrefs - a
+         * denial of service vector, since both are attacker-controlled.
+         *
+         * Nodes are collected rather than detached here because detaching mid-iteration would
+         * truncate the walk.
+         */
         foreach ($document->iterator(NodeIterator::FLAG_BLOCKS_ONLY) as $node) {
             if (! $node instanceof Footnote) {
                 continue;
             }
 
+            $label = $node->getReference()->getLabel();
+            if (isset($definitions[$label])) {
+                $discarded[] = $node;
+
+                continue;
+            }
+
+            $definitions[$label] = $node;
+        }
+
+        foreach ($definitions as $node) {
             // Look for existing reference with footnote label
             $ref = $document->getReferenceMap()->get($node->getReference()->getLabel());
             if ($ref !== null) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to deduplicate footnote definitions. It now only processes the first definition for any given label, discarding subsequent duplicates. Additionally, the storage of backreferences was refactored to prevent distinct user-supplied labels from collapsing or nesting due to internal key path delimiters, ensuring proper isolation and preventing unintended data overwrites.

## GHSA-29pj-957v-52mc · league/commonmark

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the URL sanitization logic in `isLinkPotentiallyUnsafe` did not properly handle embedded control bytes (like tabs, newlines, carriage returns, and other ASCII control characters) at the beginning or within the URL string. Browsers would strip these characters before interpreting the URL scheme, allowing an attacker to bypass the safe protocol checks and inject malicious `javascript:` URLs.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious link that, when clicked, would execute arbitrary JavaScript code in the user's browser, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow for session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to phishing sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Util/RegexHelper.php
+++ b/src/Util/RegexHelper.php
@@ -238,6 +238,11 @@ public static function getHtmlBlockCloseRegex(int $type): string
      */
     public static function isLinkPotentiallyUnsafe(string $url): bool
     {
+        // Browsers discard these bytes before resolving the scheme
+        $url = \ltrim(\str_replace(["\t", "\n", "\r"], '', $url), "\x00..\x20");
+
         return \preg_match(self::REGEX_UNSAFE_PROTOCOL, $url) !== 0 && \preg_match(self::REGEX_SAFE_DATA_PROTOCOL, $url) === 0;
     }
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by adding a preprocessing step to the `isLinkPotentiallyUnsafe` function. It now removes tab, newline, and carriage return characters from the URL and then trims all ASCII control characters (0x00-0x20) from the beginning of the string before performing the protocol safety checks. This ensures that embedded control bytes cannot be used to bypass the sanitization.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 98 occurrences**

## GHSA-w3cp-g2pf-65wh · silverstripe/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because user-supplied content, specifically page titles (MenuTitle or Title), was directly concatenated into an HTML string for breadcrumbs without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject malicious scripts if they could control the page titles.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the CMS page list view, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection of legitimate users within the CMS context.

### Key diff

```
-                $breadcrumbs = implode('/', $pageNames);
-                if ($breadcrumbs) {
-                    $breadcrumbs .= '/';
-                }
-                return $title . sprintf('<p class="small cms-list__item-breadcrumbs">%s</p>', $breadcrumbs);
+                return $title . $this->getListViewBreadcrumbs($item);
```
```php
    private function getListViewBreadcrumbs(DataObject $item): string
    {
        $pageNames = [];
        foreach ($item->getAncestors() as $page) {
            $pageNames[] = Convert::raw2xml($page->MenuTitle ?: $page->Title);
        }
        $breadcrumbs = implode('/', $pageNames);
        if ($breadcrumbs) {
            $breadcrumbs .= '/';
        }
        return sprintf('<p class="small cms-list__item-breadcrumbs">%s</p>', $breadcrumbs);
    }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new private method, `getListViewBreadcrumbs`, which explicitly escapes the page titles using `Convert::raw2xml()` before they are included in the breadcrumb HTML. The original inline logic was replaced with a call to this new, secure method.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 98 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, PHP, Java, Go, RUST, Ruby
> → GHSA-8wrq-fv5f-pfp2
> → GHSA-95h2-gj7x-gx9w
> → GHSA-rqp3-gf5h-mrqx

## GHSA-47pj-3jcm-6whg · langgraph-checkpoint-sqlite

2026-08-06 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incorrect implementation of namespace prefix matching in the SQLite and Postgres stores. The original implementation used SQL LIKE with a trailing wildcard (e.g., `prefix LIKE 'foo%'`), which matched any string starting with 'foo', including 'foobar'. This allowed queries intended for a specific namespace (e.g., 'foo') to inadvertently retrieve data from a different, longer namespace (e.g., 'foobar'), violating intended access boundaries.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially retrieve data from namespaces they were not authorized to access if those namespaces shared a common prefix with an authorized namespace. This could lead to unintended information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/libs/checkpoint-sqlite/langgraph/store/sqlite/base.py
+++ b/libs/checkpoint-sqlite/langgraph/store/sqlite/base.py
-                    WHERE prefix LIKE ?
-                params = [f"{_namespace_to_text(op.namespace_prefix)}%"]
+                    WHERE {ns_condition}
+                params = list(ns_args_tuple)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a more robust namespace matching mechanism. For SQLite, it replaces `LIKE` with a combination of exact match (`=`) and `GLOB` for direct descendants, and a custom SQLite user-defined function (`_langgraph_namespace_match`) for more complex wildcard patterns. This ensures that prefix matching respects segment boundaries, preventing unintended matches across different logical namespaces. The Postgres store also received similar logic updates.

## GHSA-6hr6-w5qg-qmwg · h2

2026-08-06 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the h2 library did not explicitly check for or reject multiple 'Host' headers in an HTTP/2 request. This allowed an attacker to send a request with duplicate Host headers, which could be interpreted differently by a frontend proxy and a backend server.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this to perform request smuggling, potentially bypassing security controls, accessing unauthorized resources, or poisoning web caches.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/h2/utilities.py
+++ b/src/h2/utilities.py
@@ -449,6 +449,9 @@ def _validate_host_authority_header(headers: Iterable[Header]) -> Generator[Head
         if header[0] == b":authority":
             authority_header_val = header[1]
         elif header[0] == b"host":
+            if host_header_val is not None:
+                msg = "Request header block has multiple Host headers."
+                raise ProtocolError(msg)
             host_header_val = header[1]
 
         yield header
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check within the `_validate_host_authority_header` function to detect if a 'Host' header has already been encountered. If a second 'Host' header is found, a `ProtocolError` is raised, preventing the request from being processed.

## GHSA-mj63-m3rc-8ppr · league/commonmark

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The XML renderer did not limit the indentation level for deeply nested XML elements. When processing a document with extreme nesting, the `str_repeat` function would generate an excessively long string for indentation, leading to quadratic memory consumption and CPU usage relative to the document's depth.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted input with deeply nested elements, causing the application to consume excessive memory and CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Xml/XmlRenderer.php
+++ b/src/Xml/XmlRenderer.php
@@ -52,10 +56,10 @@
             if ($event->isEntering()) {
                 $attrs = $renderer->getXmlAttributes($node);
 
-                $xml .= "\n" . \str_repeat(self::INDENTATION, $indent);
+                $xml .= "\n" . \str_repeat(self::INDENTATION, \min($indent, $maxIndent));
                 $xml .= self::tag($tagName, $attrs, $selfClosing);
 
                 if ($node instanceof StringContainerInterface) {
@@ -68,7 +72,7 @@
                 }
             } elseif (! $closeImmediately) {
                 $indent--;
-                $xml .= "\n" . \str_repeat(self::INDENTATION, $indent);
+                $xml .= "\n" . \str_repeat(self::INDENTATION, \min($indent, $maxIndent));
                 $xml .= self::tag('/' . $tagName);
             }
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a configuration option `xml/max_indentation_level` with a default value of 16. The `XmlRenderer` now caps the indentation level using `min($indent, $maxIndent)` to prevent the generation of excessively long indentation strings, thus limiting resource consumption.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 132 occurrences**

## GHSA-j4r3-hg7j-8chg · re2

2026-08-06 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.1 · Pattern: `BUFFER_OVERFLOW→HEAP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `node-re2` library, when processing `Buffer` inputs that ended in a truncated multi-byte UTF-8 character, could miscalculate character sizes during `replace` or `split` operations. This led to an attempt to read beyond the allocated buffer boundary.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a specific `Buffer` input that, when processed by the vulnerable functions, would cause the application to read adjacent heap memory. This memory could then be disclosed to JavaScript, potentially revealing sensitive information.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/addon.cc
+++ b/lib/addon.cc
@@ -177,6 +177,7 @@ const StrVal &WrappedRE2::prepareArgument(const v8::Local<v8::Value> &arg, bool
 		lastString.Reset(arg);
 
 		auto argSize = node::Buffer::Length(arg);
+		// isAscii stays false: every consumer branches on isBuffer first, so computing it would be a dead O(n) scan.
 		lastStringValue.reset(arg, argSize, argSize, startFrom, true);
 
 		return lastStringValue;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces bounds-aware clamping for UTF-8 character size calculations, specifically addressing the `getUtf8CharSize()` function. This ensures that character size calculations do not read beyond the buffer's boundaries, preventing the out-of-bounds read.

## GHSA-pmhh-3w7g-xqp8 · org.jsoup:jsoup

2026-08-06 · Java · **MODERATE** 4.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The jsoup HTML cleaner, when configured with custom raw-text elements, did not correctly normalize tag names. Specifically, it failed to trim whitespace from tag names provided via the public API, allowing an attacker to craft malicious input like `<script >` which would bypass cleaning rules and be treated as a raw-text element, thus preserving its content.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary HTML and JavaScript into the sanitized output, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) attacks on users viewing the processed content. This could allow session hijacking, defacement, or redirection.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/org/jsoup/parser/TagSet.java
+++ b/src/main/java/org/jsoup/parser/TagSet.java
-        tagName = tagName.trim();
+        if (normalName == null) tagName = tagName.trim(); // public API input; tokenizer names are already delimited

--- a/src/main/java/org/jsoup/parser/Token.java
+++ b/src/main/java/org/jsoup/parser/Token.java
-            normalName = ParseSettings.normalName(tagName.value());
+            normalName = lowerCase(tagName.value());
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that tag names are consistently normalized by trimming whitespace when they are provided via the public API. It also standardizes the normalization process for internal token handling to always use `lowerCase()` instead of `ParseSettings.normalName()`, which might have had different trimming behavior, thus preventing bypasses of the cleaner rules.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 98 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, PHP, Java, Go, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-9p7c-v5x3-rfx8 · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 4.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The `actionReorderSets` method in `GlobalsController.php` lacked an authorization check, specifically `requireAdmin()`. This allowed any authenticated control panel user, regardless of their administrative privileges, to execute the reordering logic for Global Sets.

### Impact

An attacker, who is a non-admin control panel user, could reorder Global Sets, potentially disrupting the content structure or presentation of the website.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/controllers/GlobalsController.php
+++ b/src/controllers/GlobalsController.php
@@ -108,6 +108,7 @@ public function actionReorderSets(): Response
     {
         $this->requirePostRequest();
         $this->requireAcceptsJson();
+        $this->requireAdmin();
 
         $setIds = Json::decode($this->request->getRequiredBodyParam('ids'));
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by adding a call to `$this->requireAdmin()` at the beginning of the `actionReorderSets` method. This ensures that only users with administrative privileges can execute the reordering functionality for Global Sets.

## GHSA-265m-7826-wjqm · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `condition.config` parameter, which could contain JSON, was not properly cleansed before being used to instantiate objects. An attacker could bypass existing JSON cleansing by providing a specially crafted `config` array that would be merged with the decoded JSON, allowing arbitrary class instantiation and method calls.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) on the server by manipulating the `condition.config` parameter to instantiate malicious objects and execute arbitrary code.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/services/Conditions.php
+++ b/src/services/Conditions.php
@@ -54,7 +55,7 @@ public function createCondition(array|string $config): ConditionInterface
         // The base config will be JSON-encoded within a `config` key if this came from a condition builder
         if (isset($config['config']) && Json::isJsonObject($config['config'])) {
             $config = array_merge(
-                Json::decode(ArrayHelper::remove($config, 'config')),
+                ComponentHelper::cleanseConfig(Json::decode(ArrayHelper::remove($config, 'config'))),
                 $config
             );
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces calls to `ComponentHelper::cleanseConfig()` on the decoded JSON configuration for conditions and condition rules. This ensures that potentially dangerous configuration values, such as `class` and `method` properties, are removed before object instantiation, preventing arbitrary code execution.

## GHSA-2rp4-x2j7-qmcc · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `draftName` of an element, which could be controlled by an attacker, was directly rendered into the HTML of the Craft CMS control panel without proper HTML encoding. This allowed for the injection of malicious scripts.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the control panel, leading to actions like stealing session cookies, performing actions on behalf of the logged-in user, or defacing the control panel interface.

### Key diff

```
-            $content .= Html::tag('span', $element->draftName ?: Craft::t('app', 'Draft'), [
-                'class' => 'context-label',
-            ]);
+            $content .= Html::tag(
+                'span',
+                $element->draftName ? Html::encode($element->draftName) : Craft::t('app', 'Draft'),
+                ['class' => 'context-label'],
+            );
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by applying HTML encoding to the `draftName` before it is rendered within a `<span>` tag in the control panel. This ensures that any special HTML characters in the draft name are escaped, preventing script injection.

## GHSA-3rrr-jr9j-h3q3 · mermaid

2026-08-06 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The original code used plain JavaScript objects (`Record<string, T>`) for storing various data structures like nodes, groups, and registered IDs. When user-controlled input was used as keys for these objects, an attacker could inject special property names like `__proto__` or `constructor.prototype` to modify the prototype chain of `Object`, leading to prototype pollution.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary properties into the `Object.prototype`, potentially leading to denial of service, remote code execution (in specific contexts), or other unexpected behavior depending on how the polluted properties are later used in the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/mermaid/src/diagrams/architecture/architectureDb.ts
+++ b/packages/mermaid/src/diagrams/architecture/architectureDb.ts
-  private nodes: Record<string, ArchitectureNode> = {};
-  private groups: Record<string, ArchitectureGroup> = {};
+  private nodes = new Map<string, ArchitectureNode>();
+  private groups = new Map<string, ArchitectureGroup>();
-  private registeredIds: Record<string, 'node' | 'group'> = {};
+  private registeredIds = new Map<string, 'node' | 'group'>();
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces all instances of plain JavaScript objects (`Record<string, T>`) used for storing collections with `Map` objects. `Map` objects do not inherit from `Object.prototype` and treat all keys as literal strings, preventing prototype pollution attacks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 25 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript
> → GHSA-fw9q-39r9-c252
> → GHSA-rv5g-f82m-qrvv

## GHSA-8rxv-jg7p-wvg3 · github.com/traefik/traefik/v3

2026-08-06 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from Traefik's handling of URL paths, specifically when processing `RewriteTarget` and NGINX snippet directives. An attacker could craft a URL with an encoded path traversal sequence (e.g., `%2f..%2f`) that, after initial decoding and rewriting, would bypass the intended path normalization and security checks. This allowed the rewritten path to escape the expected directory and access other routes.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass route-level authentication or authorization rules by manipulating the URL path, gaining unauthorized access to protected resources or endpoints that would otherwise be restricted.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/middlewares/ingressnginx/snippet/action.go
+++ b/pkg/middlewares/ingressnginx/snippet/action.go
@@ -795,6 +795,18 @@ func createRewriteAction(d config.IDirective) (action, error) {
 		// Otherwise, keep the original query string.
 	}
 
+	// Here we are sanitizing the URL when the path is not empty,
+	// as the JoinPath method is adding a leading slash if the path is empty.
+	rewrittenPath := req.URL.Path
+	if rewrittenPath != "" {
+		req.URL = req.URL.JoinPath()
+	}
+	// Stop here if the normalization of the path produces a different path.
+	if rewrittenPath != req.URL.Path {
+		ctx.statusCode = http.StatusBadRequest
+		return true, nil
+	}
+
 	req.RequestURI = req.URL.RequestURI()
 
 	// In NGINX, last restarts location matching while break stays in the
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit URL path sanitization and normalization checks. Before processing the rewritten URL, it normalizes the path using `req.URL.JoinPath()` and then compares the original path with the normalized one. If they differ, indicating a path traversal attempt, the request is rejected with an HTTP 400 Bad Request status.

## GHSA-957r-qf9p-67xw · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `SplFileObject` class was not included in the blocklist of classes that could be instantiated within non-sandboxed Twig template contexts. This allowed an attacker to use Twig functions to create an instance of `SplFileObject` and read arbitrary files on the server.

### Impact

An attacker with the ability to execute Twig templates in a non-sandboxed environment could read any file on the server that the PHP process had permissions to access, potentially leading to sensitive data disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/web/twig/Extension.php
+++ b/src/web/twig/Extension.php
@@ -1552,6 +1553,7 @@ public function createFunction(string|array $type, array $params = []): object
             FnStream::class,
             Process::class,
             SimpleXMLElement::class,
+            SplFileObject::class,
         ];
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by adding `SplFileObject::class` to the blocklist of classes that are forbidden from being instantiated within Twig templates. This prevents attackers from using this class to read arbitrary files.

## GHSA-f5wm-88jv-g5hx · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete Twig sandbox implementation. Specifically, the `AllowedInSandbox` attribute was applied to the `ElementInterface` interface itself, rather than individual properties and methods within concrete element classes like `User`. This allowed an attacker to call any method on an element object, including those not intended for sandbox access, leading to a sandbox escape.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker with access to Twig templating (e.g., via a custom field or template) could execute arbitrary PHP code on the server, achieving Remote Code Execution (RCE).

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/base/ElementInterface.php
+++ b/src/base/ElementInterface.php
@@ -30,7 +29,6 @@
  * @author Pixel & Tonic, Inc. <support@pixelandtonic.com>
  * @since 3.0.0
  */
-#[AllowedInSandbox]
 interface ElementInterface extends ComponentInterface
 {
     /**
--- a/src/elements/User.php
+++ b/src/elements/User.php
@@ -640,16 +640,19 @@ public static function findIdentityByAccessToken($token, $type = null): ?self
     /**
      * @var int|null Invalid login count
      */
+    #[AllowedInSandbox]
     public ?int $invalidLoginCount = null;
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by moving the `AllowedInSandbox` attribute from the `ElementInterface` to specific properties and methods within the `User` class and other concrete element implementations. This ensures that only explicitly whitelisted properties and methods are accessible within the Twig sandbox, preventing unauthorized method calls.

## GHSA-hmqg-cxww-wqhq · squizlabs/php_codesniffer

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application constructed shell commands using user-supplied filenames without proper escaping. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary shell commands by crafting a malicious filename that would be executed when the application processed it with tools like 'git blame', 'svn blame', or 'hg blame'.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the server where PHP_CodeSniffer is running, potentially leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Reports/Gitblame.php
+++ b/src/Reports/Gitblame.php
@@ -70,7 +70,7 @@ protected function getBlameContent($filename)
         $cwd = getcwd();
 
         chdir(dirname($filename));
-        $command = 'git blame --date=short "'.basename($filename).'" 2>&1';
+        $command = 'git blame --date=short -- '.escapeshellarg(basename($filename)).' 2>&1';
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the use of `escapeshellarg()` for all user-supplied filenames before they are incorporated into shell commands. This ensures that special shell characters within filenames are properly quoted, preventing them from being interpreted as commands or command arguments.

## GHSA-p8x7-9vfw-p7vc · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The application allowed certain sensitive user attributes, including 'password' and 'newPassword', to be set directly from request parameters without proper authorization checks. This meant an attacker could craft a request to update these fields for any user, including administrators.

### Impact

An attacker could reset the password of any user, including an administrator, and subsequently take over their account, gaining full control over the Craft CMS instance.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/elements/User.php
+++ b/src/elements/User.php
@@ -1072,7 +1072,18 @@ public function safeAttributes(): array
      */
     public function setAttributesFromRequest($values): void
     {
-        unset($values['unverifiedEmail']);
+        unset(
+            $values['invalidLoginCount'],
+            $values['lastInvalidLoginDate'],
+            $values['lastLoginAttemptIp'],
+            $values['lastLoginDate'],
+            $values['lastPasswordChangeDate'],
+            $values['lockoutDate'],
+            $values['newPassword'],
+            $values['password'],
+            $values['unverifiedEmail'],
+            $values['verificationCodeIssuedDate'],
+        );
```

### Fix summary

The patch explicitly blacklists a comprehensive list of sensitive user attributes, including 'password', 'newPassword', and various login-related fields, from being set directly from request parameters. This prevents unauthorized modification of these critical fields.

## GHSA-rvmm-v933-jgxq · craftcms/cms

2026-08-06 · PHP · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The `actionGetNewUsersData` method in `ChartsController.php` lacked an explicit authorization check to ensure that only control panel users with appropriate administrative privileges could access user registration metrics. This allowed any authenticated control panel user, regardless of their role, to view this sensitive data.

### Impact

An attacker, as a non-admin control panel user, could access user registration metrics, potentially leading to information disclosure about user growth and activity that should be restricted to administrators.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/controllers/ChartsController.php
+++ b/src/controllers/ChartsController.php
@@ -37,6 +37,8 @@ class ChartsController extends Controller
      */
     public function actionGetNewUsersData(): Response
     {
+        $this->requireCpRequest();
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds `$this->requireCpRequest();` at the beginning of the `actionGetNewUsersData` method. This ensures that the request originates from the control panel and implicitly enforces the necessary control panel user authentication and authorization checks before processing the request for user data.

## GHSA-vp3h-ghgh-jr7g · nx

2026-08-06 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application was extracting tarball contents from a remote cache without properly sanitizing or validating the entry paths within the archive. This allowed a malicious remote cache server to craft archive entries with paths like `../../` or absolute paths, which would cause files to be written outside the intended cache directory.

### Impact

An attacker controlling the remote cache could write arbitrary files to arbitrary locations on the file system of the machine running the Nx client, potentially leading to remote code execution or other system compromise.

### Key diff

```
-                match entry.unpack(&path_on_disk) {
-                    Err(e) => {
-                        return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Failed to unpack entry: {}", e));
-                    }
-                    Ok(f) => match f {
-                        tar::Unpacked::File(f) => size += f.metadata()?.len() as i64,
-                        _ => (),
-                    },
-                }
+                let unpacked = entry
+                    .unpack_in(&output_dir)
+                    .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("Failed to unpack entry: {}", e))?;
+                if !unpacked {
+                    return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
+                        "Refusing to extract cache entry with unsafe path: {}",
+                        entry_path
+                    ));
+                }
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the tarball extraction logic into a dedicated function and, crucially, switches from `entry.unpack()` to `entry.unpack_in()`. The `unpack_in()` method is designed to prevent path traversal by canonicalizing the target directory and rejecting entries that attempt to write outside of it, including those with `..` or absolute paths. It also explicitly checks the return value of `unpack_in` to ensure no unsafe paths were extracted.

## GHSA-x677-9fxg-v5c5 · github.com/traefik/traefik/v3

2026-08-06 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete fix for previous issues (CVE-2026-33433, CVE-2026-39858) related to how Traefik handled HTTP headers containing underscores. Different web servers and proxies normalize or interpret headers with underscores differently (e.g., converting them to hyphens or ignoring them), leading to inconsistencies that could be exploited for identity spoofing in authentication mechanisms like BasicAuth, DigestAuth, or ForwardAuth.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication or authorization mechanisms by crafting requests with specially named headers containing underscores. This could lead to unauthorized access to resources or services protected by Traefik.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/server/server_entrypoint_tcp.go
+++ b/pkg/server/server_entrypoint_tcp.go
@@ -601,6 +601,17 @@ func createHTTPServer(ctx context.Context, ln net.Listener, configuration *stati
 
 	handler = denyFragment(handler)
 
+	switch configuration.HTTP.UnderscoreHeadersStrategy {
+	case "", static.UnderscoreHeadersStrategyKeep:
+		// Headers with underscores are forwarded as is.
+	case static.UnderscoreHeadersStrategyDelete:
+		handler = removeHeadersWithUnderscores(handler)
+	case static.UnderscoreHeadersStrategyReject:
+		handler = rejectHeadersWithUnderscores(handler)
+	default:
+		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid underscoreHeadersStrategy value %q", configuration.HTTP.UnderscoreHeadersStrategy)
+	}
+
 	var connContext multipleConnContext
 	connContext.AddConnContextFunc(func(ctx context.Context, c net.Conn) context.Context {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new configuration option, `UnderscoreHeadersStrategy`, for HTTP entry points. This strategy allows administrators to define how Traefik should handle headers with underscores: `keep` (forward as is), `delete` (remove them), or `reject` (return a 400 Bad Request). By default, it's set to `keep` for backward compatibility, but `delete` or `reject` can be used to mitigate the spoofing risk.
